<!-- gsd-toolkit:canonical:start -->
# GSD worktree workflow

This repo uses gsd-toolkit. Its full rules come from the installed toolkit:
run `gsdt-rules` and follow its output (Claude Code loads it when a session
starts). If `gsdt-rules` is not found, install the toolkit:
https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit#install

These hold even without the toolkit (a teammate, CI, a cloud session):
- One phase = one `phase-<N>-<slug>` git worktree; the main checkout stays
  on the base branch.
- Run `gsdt-flow-next <N>` before every phase action and follow it.
- `gsdt-finish` is never automatic: run it only when the user asks, as the
  last action (it removes the worktree).
- Never bypass a guard unless the user explicitly asks for `GSD_SKIP_GUARD=1`.

Providers: `codex` (default: first, `codex`); base `main`;
worktrees `../shop-worktrees/`.
<!-- gsd-toolkit:canonical:end -->

── .gsd.conf [-]
# .gsd.conf â committed, inspectable GSD worktree configuration.
base = main
wtdir = shop-worktrees
install = none
providers = codex
# start_mode = discuss  # optional; default is flow (gsdt-start opens /gsdt-flow)
# flow = strict
# ui_gates = strict  # UI gates: warn (default), strict, off

── AGENTS.md [-]

<!-- gsd-toolkit:provider:start -->
## GSD worktrees (codex adapter)

Read and follow `.gsd/INSTRUCTIONS.md` (this repo's settings and core rules).
Before any GSD or phase work, run `gsdt-rules` and follow its output: it prints
the toolkit's rules for the installed version.
Use the `gsdt-flow` skill when it is available; otherwise run
`gsdt-flow-next <N>` and follow each printed action.
For toolkit command syntax and workflow order, this marked adapter and the
canonical document supersede older unmarked GSD text in this file. Continue to
follow repository-specific policy from that older text.
<!-- gsd-toolkit:provider:end -->

── README.md [-]
hello

── scripts/gsdt-derive-port.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-derive-port). Referenced from app package.json dev scripts, so it
# degrades gracefully: without the toolkit (CI, a teammate's machine) it
# prints the bare base port and plain `dev` keeps working.
command -v gsdt-derive-port >/dev/null 2>&1 && exec gsdt-derive-port "$@"
echo "${1:?usage: gsdt-derive-port.sh <base-port>}"

── scripts/gsdt-finish-worktree-feature.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-wt-finish). This file exists so committed references keep working
# and never needs updating. Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.
command -v gsdt-wt-finish >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || { echo "error: gsdt-wt-finish not found â install gsd-toolkit (https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit)" >&2; exit 1; }
exec gsdt-wt-finish "$@"

── scripts/gsdt-new-worktree-feature.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-wt-new). This file exists so committed references keep working
# and never needs updating. Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.
command -v gsdt-wt-new >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || { echo "error: gsdt-wt-new not found â install gsd-toolkit (https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit)" >&2; exit 1; }
exec gsdt-wt-new "$@"

── scripts/gsdt-planning-repair.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-planning-repair). This file exists so committed references (CI
# jobs, git hooks, package.json scripts) keep working and never need updating.
# Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.
command -v gsdt-planning-repair >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || { echo "error: gsdt-planning-repair not found â install gsd-toolkit (https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit)" >&2; exit 1; }
exec gsdt-planning-repair "$@"

── scripts/hooks/gsdt-worktree-guard.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real guard lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-worktree-guard). Provider adapters may register this defense-in-depth
# native hook; the supported shell flow calls the shared guard directly. This
# shim fails open when the toolkit is missing.
command -v gsdt-worktree-guard >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0
exec gsdt-worktree-guard "$@"

── post-merge hook [x]
#!/usr/bin/env sh
# Installed by gsdt-bootstrap-repo (gsd-toolkit).
# .planning/ROADMAP.md and STATE.md merge with a union-then-reconcile driver.
# A pull whose driver was missing, or a merge done by another tool, can still
# leave both sides of a single-value line behind â reconcile them here.
command -v gsdt-planning-repair >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0
[ -d .planning ] || exit 0
gsdt-planning-repair || true

<!-- gsd-toolkit:canonical:start -->
# GSD worktree workflow

This repo uses gsd-toolkit. Its full rules come from the installed toolkit:
run `gsdt-rules` and follow its output (Claude Code loads it when a session
starts). If `gsdt-rules` is not found, install the toolkit:
https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit#install

These hold even without the toolkit (a teammate, CI, a cloud session):
- One phase = one `phase-<N>-<slug>` git worktree; the main checkout stays
  on the base branch.
- Run `gsdt-flow-next <N>` before every phase action and follow it.
- `gsdt-finish` is never automatic: run it only when the user asks, as the
  last action (it removes the worktree).
- Never bypass a guard unless the user explicitly asks for `GSD_SKIP_GUARD=1`.

Providers: `claude` (default: first, `claude`); base `main`;
worktrees `../shop-worktrees/`.
<!-- gsd-toolkit:canonical:end -->

── .gsd.conf [-]
# .gsd.conf â committed, inspectable GSD worktree configuration.
base = main
wtdir = shop-worktrees
install = none
providers = claude
# start_mode = discuss  # optional; default is flow (gsdt-start opens /gsdt-flow)
# flow = strict
# ui_gates = strict  # UI gates: warn (default), strict, off

── CLAUDE.md [-]

<!-- gsd-toolkit:provider:start -->
## GSD worktrees (claude adapter)

Read and follow `.gsd/INSTRUCTIONS.md` (this repo's settings and core rules).
The toolkit's rules are the output of `gsdt-rules`: a session-start hook loads
them ("GSD toolkit rules"); when they are not in your context, run `gsdt-rules`
before any GSD or phase work and follow its output.
Use the `gsdt-flow` skill when it is available; otherwise run
`gsdt-flow-next <N>` and follow each printed action.
For toolkit command syntax and workflow order, this marked adapter and the
canonical document supersede older unmarked GSD text in this file. Continue to
follow repository-specific policy from that older text.
<!-- gsd-toolkit:provider:end -->

── README.md [-]
hello

── scripts/gsdt-derive-port.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-derive-port). Referenced from app package.json dev scripts, so it
# degrades gracefully: without the toolkit (CI, a teammate's machine) it
# prints the bare base port and plain `dev` keeps working.
command -v gsdt-derive-port >/dev/null 2>&1 && exec gsdt-derive-port "$@"
echo "${1:?usage: gsdt-derive-port.sh <base-port>}"

── scripts/gsdt-finish-worktree-feature.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-wt-finish). This file exists so committed references keep working
# and never needs updating. Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.
command -v gsdt-wt-finish >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || { echo "error: gsdt-wt-finish not found â install gsd-toolkit (https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit)" >&2; exit 1; }
exec gsdt-wt-finish "$@"

── scripts/gsdt-new-worktree-feature.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-wt-new). This file exists so committed references keep working
# and never needs updating. Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.

── scripts/gsdt-planning-repair.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real logic lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-planning-repair). This file exists so committed references (CI
# jobs, git hooks, package.json scripts) keep working and never need updating.
# Repo settings: .gsd.conf at the repo root.
command -v gsdt-planning-repair >/dev/null 2>&1 \
  || { echo "error: gsdt-planning-repair not found â install gsd-toolkit (https://github.com/malyeg/gsd-toolkit)" >&2; exit 1; }
exec gsdt-planning-repair "$@"

── scripts/hooks/gsdt-worktree-guard.sh [x]
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# gsd-toolkit shim â the real guard lives in the gsd-toolkit package
# (bin/gsdt-worktree-guard). Provider adapters may register this defense-in-depth
# native hook; the supported shell flow calls the shared guard directly. This
# shim fails open when the toolkit is missing.
command -v gsdt-worktree-guard >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0
exec gsdt-worktree-guard "$@"

── post-merge hook [x]
#!/usr/bin/env sh
# Installed by gsdt-bootstrap-repo (gsd-toolkit).
# .planning/ROADMAP.md and STATE.md merge with a union-then-reconcile driver.
# A pull whose driver was missing, or a merge done by another tool, can still
# leave both sides of a single-value line behind â reconcile them here.
command -v gsdt-planning-repair >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 0
[ -d .planning ] || exit 0
gsdt-planning-repair || true
